\textsf{wt}\xi_{4}(T)=r_{4}.\textsf{wt}(T)=r_{4}(-2,1,-1,-1)=(-2,1,-1,1).

Proposition 7
.

Let
B
(λ)
be a type
C_{n}
crystal,
J\subseteq I
and
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Bj}{{{}}\textsf{B}_{J}}=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{Bj}{{{}}\textsf{B}_{J}}(\lambda)
. Let
b\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{cncrystal}{{{}}\textsf{B}(\lambda)}
. The connected component of

B

J

containing
b
has highest weight element
b_{J}^{\textsf{high}}
and lowest weight element
b_{J}^{\textsf{low}}
. Then

(1)

b_{J}^{\textsf{low}}=r_{a}\cdots r_{d}.b_{J}^{\textsf{high}}=\xi_{a}\cdots\xi_{d}(b_{J}^{\textsf{high}})
where
r_{a}\cdots r_{d}
is a
short word for
w_{0}^{J}\in W^{J}
with
a,\dots,d\in J
, and
b=f_{j_{r}}\cdots f_{j_{1}}(b_{J}^{\textsf{high}})
for some

j_{r},\dots,{j_{1}}\in J
.

(2)

If
J=[p,n]
,
\textsf{B}_{[p,n]}
is a type
C_{n-p+1}
crystal, then

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xij}{{{}}\xi_{\textsf{J}}}(b)=e_{j_{r}}\cdots e_{j_{1}}(r_{a}\cdots r_{d}.b_{J}^{\textsf{high}}),\;\textsf{wt}_{J}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xij}{{{}}\xi_{\textsf{J}}}(b))=-\textsf{wt}_{J}(b),

where
\textsf{wt}_{J}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-p+1}
,
x\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{B}{{{}}\textsf{B}}
, denotes
\textsf{wt}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
restricted to the
entries in
[p,n]
.

(3)

If
J=[p,q]
,
1\leq p\leq q<n
,
\textsf{B}_{[p,q]}
is a type
A_{q-p+1}
, crystal, and

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xij}{{{}}\xi_{\textsf{J}}}(b)=e_{q-p-j_{r}+1}\cdots e_{q-p-j_{1}+1}(r_{a}\cdots r_{d}.b_{J}^{\textsf{high}}),\;\textsf{wt}_{J}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xij}{{{}}\xi_{\textsf{J}}}(b))=\textsf{reverse}(\textsf{wt}_{J}(b)),

where
\textsf{wt}_{J}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}^{q-p+1}
,
x\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{B}{{{}}\textsf{B}}
, denotes
\textsf{wt}(x)\in\mathbb{Z}^{n}
restricted to the
entries in
[p,q+1]
.

9.2.
Dynkin sub-diagram
J=[j,n]
: J-symplectic reversal

On the set
\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)
,
\xi^{C_{n}}=\xi^{C_{n}}_{[1,n]}
coincides with
Santos’ symplectic evacuation
\textsf{evac}^{C_{n}}
(see
7.4.1
or
[
sa21a
, Section5]
). The partial Schützenberger–Lusztig involution
\xi^{C_{n}}_{[j,n]}
is the Schützenberger–Lusztig involution on each connected component of
\textsf{KN}_{[j,n]}(\lambda,n)
.

9.2.1.
The action of the Knuth operator
\bf R3
on a skew tableau

Given
1<j\leq n
, the Levi branched crystal
\textsf{KN}_{[j,n]}(\lambda,n)
decomposes into connected components. Let
T\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)}
, which belongs to some connected component of

\textsf{KN}_{[j,n]}(\lambda,n)
, and let
T_{[\pm j,n]}
denote the restriction of
T
to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
.
T_{[\pm j,n]}
is a KN skew tableau on the
alphabet
C

n

(Lemma
7
). However,
T_{[\pm j,n]}
might have non-admissible columns with respect to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. This means that by doing a shift of
-(j-1)
to the entries of
T_{[\pm j,n]}
, we might produce a non-admissible skew tableau on the alphabet
\mathcal{C}_{n-j+1}
(recall Definition
1
and Remark
2
). We show that under the action of the contractor operator
\bf R3
,
T_{[\pm j,n]}
is symplectic Knuth equivalent to a KN skew tableau on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. Consequently, the connected component containing
T_{[\pm j,n]}
is symplectic Knuth equivalent to a crystal connected component of admissible skew tableaux on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
(of the same skew shape).

Proposition 8
.

[
lec02
, Proposition 2.3.3]
Let
C_{1},\dots,C_{k}

be admissible columns on the alphabet
C

n

. Then
T=C_{1}C_{2}\cdots C_{k}
is a KN tableau on the alphabet
C

n

if and only if
l(C_{i})\leq r(C_{i+1})
, that is, if
l(C_{i})r(C_{i+1})
is a type
A_{2n-1}
semi-standard tableau for
i=1,\dots,k-1
.

Lemma 7
.

Let
T\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)}
. The restriction of
T
to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]=[n]\setminus\{1<\cdots<j-1<\overline{j-1}<\cdots<\bar{2}<\bar{1}\}
,
T_{\pm j,n}
, is a KN skew tableau on the alphabet
C

n

where

T_{\pm j,n}
might have non-admissible columns with respect to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
.

Proof.

If a cell of
T
has a barred letter in
[\pm j-1]
, then the cells to the southeast have barred entries in
[\pm j-1]
, and if a
cell of
T
has a non-barred letter in
[\pm j-1]
, then the cells to the northwest are non-barred and belong to

[j-1]
. Therefore, the non-barred letters of
T
in
[\pm j-1]
define a partition shape, say
\mu
, in
T
, and the barred letters in

[\pm j-1]
define a skew shape
\lambda/\nu
where
\mu\subseteq\nu\subseteq\lambda
. Hence the cells of
T
filled in
[\pm j,n]=[n]\setminus\{1<\cdots<j-1<\overline{j-1}<\cdots<\bar{2}<\bar{1}\}
define the skew shape
\nu/\mu
.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Let
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
be two columns with entries on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
such that

C_{1}C_{2}
is a skew KN tableau on the alphabet
C

n

. Assume that
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
have exactly
m\geq 0
and
t\geq 0
pairs of symmetric entries
(x,\bar{x})
, respectively, with
N(x)>x
with respect to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. The columns are admissible skew tableaux on the alphabet
C

n

but not necessarily on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
when
j>1
. Then
C_{1}
has at least
m
boxes strictly below the row containing the last box of
C_{2}
, and
C_{2}
has at least
t
boxes strictly above the row containing the top box of column
C_{1}
.

Proof.

Consider
spl(C_{1}C_{2})=lC_{1}rC_{1}lC_{2}rC_{2}
in
[\pm n]
, which is a type
A_{2n-1}
semi-standard tableau on the
alphabet
C

n

. Under the lemma’s assumptions, when
m>0
,

C_{1}
is not-admissible in
[\pm j,n]
and has
m>0
pairs of symmetric entries
(\alpha_{i},\bar{\alpha}_{i})
where
N(\alpha_{i})>\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,m
; when
t>0
,
C_{2}
is not admissible in
[\pm j,n]
and has
t>0
pairs of symmetric entries

(\beta_{i},\bar{\beta}_{i})
where
N(\beta_{i})>\beta_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,t
. Therefore, from the definition of
spl(C_{1}C_{2})
, the top
box of
r(C_{1})
is filled in the interval
[\pm j,n]
, and the first
t
entries of
lC_{2}
are filled in
[j-1]
. Since

rC_{1}lC_{2}
is a type
A_{2n-1}
semi-standard tableau on the alphabet
C

n

, it follows that the first
t

entries
of column
C_{2}

are strictly above the row containing the top box of column
C_{1}
. On the other hand, from the definition of
spl(C_{1}C_{2})
, the last
m

boxes of
r(C_{1})
are filled in
\{\overline{(j-1)}<\cdots<\bar{2}<\bar{1}\}
, and the bottom box of
lC_{2}
is filled in
[\pm j,n]
.
Similarly, since
rC_{1}lC_{2}
is a type
A_{2n-1}
semi-standard tableau on the alphabet
C

n

, it follows
that the
last
m
entries of column
C_{1}
are strictly below the row containing the bottom box of column
C_{2}
.
∎

Let
(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3})^{m}
denote the iteration of the Knuth operator
\bf R3
,
m\geq 0
times.

Proposition 9
.

Let
C_{1}
,
C_{2}
be two columns on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
such that

C_{1}C_{2}
is a skew KN tableau on the alphabet
C

n

under the conditions of the previous lemma. Let

C_{1}\overset{(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3})^{m}}{\equiv}X
, where
X
is an admissible column on
[\pm j,n]
, and
C_{2}\overset{(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3})^{t}}{\equiv}Y
, where
Y
is an
admissible column on
[\pm j,n]
. Then

C_{1}C_{2}\overset{(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3})^{m}}{\equiv}XC_{2}\overset{(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3})^{t}}{\equiv}XY
is a skew KN tableau on
[\pm j,n]
.

Proof.

Under our assumptions,

C_{1}
has
m\geq 0
pairs of symmetric entries
(\alpha_{i},\bar{\alpha}_{i})
, where
N(\alpha_{i})>\alpha_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,m
, with
respect to the interval
[\pm j,n]
(a column is non-admissible on
[\pm j,n]
if
m>0
), and the
\bf R3
contraction is applied
m\geq 0

times to
C_{1}
,
C_{1}\overset{(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3})^{m}}{\equiv}X
, where
X
is admissible on
[\pm j,n]
. Henceforth, after applying the contraction

R3

m
times to
C_{1}
, the relevant
m
pairs of symmetric entries are deleted, the top
m
entries and the bottom
m
entries of
column
C_{1}
are made empty and the
remaining entries of
C_{1}
are put in order in the remaining
|C_{1}|-2m
boxes of
C_{1}
to define the admissible column
X
on
[\pm j,n]
.
Similarly, under our assumptions about
C_{2}
,
C_{2}
has
t\geq 0
pairs of symmetric entries
(\beta_{i},\bar{\beta}_{i})
such that
N(\beta_{i})>\beta_{i}
,
i=1,\dots,t
, with respect to the interval
[\pm j,n]
. After
applying the contraction
R3

t
to
C_{2}
, the relevant
t
pairs of symmetric entries are deleted, the top
t
entries and the bottom

t
entries of column
C_{2}
are made empty and the remaining entries of
C_{2}
are put in order in the remaining
|C_{2}|-2t
boxes of
C_{2}

to define the admissible column
Y
on
[\pm j,n]
. Thus, from Lemma
8
, the resulting pair
XY
of admissible columns has skew
shape.

Moreover,
XY
is a KN skew tableau on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
, that is,
rX\,lY
, with entries on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
, is a
type

A_{2n-1}
semi-standard tableau. By definition of
spl(X)spl(Y)
,
rX\,lY
has the same skew shape as
XY
. Note that

rX=rC_{1}\setminus(\{\alpha_{1},\dots\alpha_{m}\}\sqcup[\pm j-1])

is obtained from
rC_{1}
by emptying the top
m
boxes and
bottom
m

boxes of
rC_{1}
and by filling in order the remaining boxes of
rC_{1}
with
rC_{1}\setminus(\{\alpha_{1},\dots\alpha_{m}\}\sqcup[\pm j-1])
. Indeed, from Lemma
8
,

rX\leq lC_{2}\setminus[j-1],

and in particular,
rXlC_{2}\setminus[j-1])
is a semi-standard tableau. Recall that the top box of
rC_{1}
is strictly below
the top

t
boxes of
rC_{2}
(exactly the ones in
lC_{2}
filled in
[j-1]
), and the bottom box of
lC_{2}
is strictly above the
bottom
m

boxes of
rC_{1}
(exactly the ones in
rC_{1}
filled in
\overline{(j-1)}<\cdots<\bar{1}
).

Finally, note that

lY=lC_{2}\setminus([j-1]\sqcup\{\bar{\beta}_{1},\dots,\bar{\beta}_{t}\}),

and if
rXlC_{2}\setminus[j-1])
is a semi-standard tableau, then
rXl(C_{2}\setminus([j-1]\sqcup\{\bar{\beta}_{1},\dots,\bar{\beta}_{t}\})=rX\,lY
is also a semi-standard tableau.
∎

9.2.2.
Reduced symplectic jeu de taquin

Given
T\in\textsf{KN}(\lambda/\mu,n)
and
j\in[n]
such that
T
has all entries in
[\pm j,n]
, the following is an algorithm to compute the
reduced symplectic jeu de taquin
on
T
on the interval
[\pm j,n]
, denoted
SJDT_{j}
. The skew tableau
T
might not be admissible on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. This means that we apply the SJDT after shifting all entries in
T
by
-(j-1)
and iterating on
T
the contractor operator
R3
the needed number of times to get an admissible skew tableau on the alphabet
\mathcal{C}_{n-j+1}
. When
j=1
, we recover the ordinary SJDT.

Definition 4
.

Reduced SJDT (
SJDT_{j}
)

•

Let
T_{j}
be the tableau obtained by replacing each non-barred entry
c
and barred entry
\bar{c}
in
T
by
c-j+1
and
\overline{c-j+1}
, respectively.

•

If
T_{j}
is not a KN tableau in
\textsf{KN}(\lambda/\mu,n-j+1)
, we have some columns containing pairs of the form
b,\overline{b}
such that
b\in[n-j+1]
is lowest in the column and
N(b)>b
. Iteratively, we apply the Knuth contractor
R3
operator to
T_{j}
until we make all columns admissible. Define
T_{j}
to be the resulting tableau with all admissible columns.

•

Compute SJDT on
T_{j}
as usual.

•

Replace each non-barred entry
m
and
\bar{m}
in
SJDT(T_{j})
by
m+j-1
and
\overline{m-j+1}
, respectively.

The
reduced rectification
to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
, denoted

\textsf{rectification}_{j}
(
\textsf{rect}_{j}
), of
T
is the iteration
of the
SJDT_{j}
to all inner corners in
T
. Indeed,

\textsf{rect}_{j}(T)
is the shift by
j-1
of all entries of

\textsf{rect}(T_{j})
. When
j=1
we recover the ordinary rectification.

[\pm 1,3]

9.2.3.
Partial symplectic reversal: colorful symplectic tableau switching.

Let
T\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)}
and
j\geq 1
. Let
B
be the crystal connected component of
\textsf{KN}_{[j,n]}(\lambda,n)
containing

T
.
B
is a highest weight crystal and all vertices of
B
are KN tableaux on the alphabet
C

n

, with the
letters in
[\pm j-1]
frozen, as the crystal operators in
B
are indexed by
[j,n]
and do not act on the entries filled in
[\pm j-1]
.

Let
H
be the highest weight element of
B
, and let
\textsf{wt}(H_{[\pm j,n]})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n-j+1}
be its highest weight,
where
H_{[\pm j,n]}
is the restriction of
H
to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. The restriction of
H
to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
is a skew KN
tableau on the alphabet
C

n

.
The entries of
H
in
[j-1]
define a semi-standard tableau
T^{+}_{[j-1]}
of shape, say
\mu
, and the entries in
[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]
define a
skew semi-standard tableau
T^{-}_{[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]}
of shape
\lambda/\nu
, where
\mu\subseteq\nu\subseteq\lambda
. Hence
the cells of
H
filled in

[\pm j,n]=[n]\setminus\{1<\cdots<j-1<\overline{j-1}<\cdots<\bar{2}<\bar{1}\}
define the skew shape
\nu/\mu
, and because the crystal operators in
B

are indexed by
[j,n]
, they do not change the skew shape
\nu/\mu
either. Therefore, since all the vertices of
B
are connected
to
H
through those crystal operators, the vertices of
B
restricted to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
have the same skew shape
\nu/\mu

and the same semi-standard tableaux
T^{+}_{[j-1]}
and
T^{-}_{[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]}

[
lec02
, Lemma 6.1.3]
.

Step I
.
The sequence of isomorphic crystals from
T_{[\pm j,n]}
to its reduced rectification.

I.1
-
The
C_{n-j+1}
connected crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
containing
T_{[\pm j,n]}
.

Erase in the vertices of
B
the entries in
[\pm j-1]
; that is, erase the semi-standard tableaux
T^{+}_{[j-1]}
and
T^{-}_{[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]}
. We obtain the connected
C_{n-j+1}
crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
of semi-standard skew tableaux of shape
\nu/\mu
with entries in the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
,
possibly with some non-admissible columns, containing
T_{[\pm j,n]}
. These KN skew tableaux over
C

n

might have non-admissible columns over
[\pm j,n]
. More precisely,
\textsf{B}^{0}
is the connected crystal of words on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
, with highest element the word
of
H_{[\pm j,n]}
.

The set
\textsf{B}^{0}
bijects the set
B
, with the same crystal graph structure and the same weight vertices as

B
. Hence,
\textsf{B}^{0}
and
B
are isomorphic crystals.

I.1.1
-
The green inner standard tableau
U_{0}
for any vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
.

Define a standard tableau
U_{0}
of shape
\mu
filled in a completely ordered alphabet of
green letters

\{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{|\mu|}}\}
where
|\mu|
is the number of boxes of
\mu
.
Assign the inner standard tableau
U_{0}
the inner shape of each vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
. Recall
T_{[\pm j,n]}
is the image of
T
in
\textsf{B}^{0}
; see the tableau pair
(U_{0},T_{[\pm j,n]})
in Figure
1
.

(U_{0},T_{[\pm j,n]})=

\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\bullet
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\dots
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\bullet
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\bullet
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\bullet
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\vdots
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\ddots
\color[rgb]{0,1,0}\bullet
T_{[\pm j,n]}

Figure 1.
T_{[\pm j,n]}
in the crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
and the inner tableau
U_{0}
.

I.2
-
The
C_{n-j+1}
crystal
\textsf{B}^{x}
of KN skew tableaux
R3
isomorphic to
\textsf{B}^{0}
.

Let
H^{0}:=H_{[\pm j,n]}
be the highest weight element of the
C_{n-j+1}
crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
.
The skew tableau

H^{0}
of shape
\nu/\mu
may have non-admissible columns on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. Let
r<s<\dots<q<t

be the non-admissible columns of
H^{0}
. Then exactly the same columns in all vertices of
\textsf{B}^{0}
are non-admissible.
The Knuth contraction
R3
relation, Subsection
R3
, defines a crystal isomorphism; it commutes with the crystal
operators and preserves the weight. Moreover, each time
R3
is applied to a column of some vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
, it is also
applied to the same column in every vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
(see
[
lec02
, Proposition 3.2.4, Corollary 3.2.5]
).

In each vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
, apply the
R3
contraction operation to column
i
, for
i=r,s,\dots,q,t
,
until column
i
becomes admissible. For
i=r,s,\dots,q,t
, each time we apply
R3
to column
i
, a pair
of entries
(k,\bar{k})
is erased (whenever
k\in[n]
is minimal for
N(k)>k
,
k
and
\bar{k}
appear in the column and all prefixes are admissible).
Then the cells from the top and the bottom of the current column
i
are emptied; the remaining entries are placed in order in the remaining cells between those erased. We obtain a new crystal of KN skew tableaux on the alphabet

[\pm j,n]
isomorphic
to the crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
.

Let
x
be the total times
R3
has to be applied to
H^{0}
, from column
r
to column
t
as explained above, to get a KN skew
tableau
on alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. Denote the resulting KN skew tableau by
H^{x}
. Note that for each
column of any vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
,
the number of times
R3
is applied is the same. We then obtain the sequence of isomorphic crystals

\displaystyle\textsf{B}^{0}\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\textsf{B}^{1}\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\cdots\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\textsf{B}^{x_{r}}\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\textsf{B}^{x_{r}+1}\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\cdots\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\textsf{B}^{x_{r}+x_{s}}

\displaystyle\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\cdots\overset{\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{r3}{{{}}\bf R3}}{\simeq}\textsf{B}^{x_{r}+x_{s}+\cdots+x_{q}+x_{t}}=\textsf{B}^{x},

where
x=x_{r}+x_{s}+\cdots+x_{q}+x_{t}
and
x_{i}
is the number of times we apply
R3
to column
i
of
H^{0}
, for

i=r,s,\dots,q,t
.
The crystal
\textsf{B}^{x}
, isomorphic to
\textsf{B}^{0}
, is obtained by applying
R3

x
times to each vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
,
namely,

x_{i}
times to column
i
, for
i=r,\dots,q,t
, of each vertex of
\textsf{B}^{x}
.
Equivalently,
\textsf{B}^{x}
is the crystal whose highest weight element is the KN skew tableau
H^{x}
of shape
\nu^{x}/\mu^{x}
, where

\nu^{x}\subseteq\nu
,
\mu\subseteq\mu^{x}
and

|\mu^{x}|-|\mu|=|\nu|-|\nu^{x}|=x
is the number of times
R3
has been applied to
H^{0}
(or
T_{[\pm j,n]}
).

I.2.1
-
The pair
(U_{x},V_{x})
of green-purple inner and purple outer standard tableaux for any vertex of
\textsf{B}^{x}
.

Let

\displaystyle\{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{|\mu|}}<{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{2}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}^{\prime}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{2}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}^{\prime}}\}

(46)

be a completely ordered alphabet of
|\mu|+2x
letters consisting of
|\mu|
green letters and
x
unprimed and
x
primed
purple letters
.

Define the standard tableau
U_{x}
of shape
\mu^{x}
, where
\mu\subseteq\mu^{x}
and
|\mu^{x}|=|\mu|+x
, to be an extension of
U_{0}

filled with the
|\mu|
green letters by filling the extra

x
cells, the total number of cells made empty at the top of each non-admissible column in a vertex of
\textsf{B}^{0}
, with the unprimed
purple letters
\{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}\}
. Define the standard tableau

V_{x}
of shape
\nu/\nu^{x}
by filling the
x
cells made empty at the bottom of each non-admissible column in a vertex of

\textsf{B}^{0}
with the primed purple
letters
{\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}^{\prime}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}^{\prime}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}^{\prime}}
. The filling rule is as follows.

Fill successively the pair of cells made empty each time
R3
is applied, with one unprimed purple letter and one primed purple letter,
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}^{\prime},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}},\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+1}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+1},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+x_{s}}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+x_{s}},\dots,\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}^{\prime}
, with the unprimed
letter at the top of the column and the primed letter at the bottom of the column. We impose the order

\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{|\mu|}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+x_{s}}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}<

<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+x_{s}}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+1}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}^{\prime}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{1}.

That is, each time an unprimed purple letter and a primed purple letter are added to
U_{x}
and
V_{x}
, respectively, the unprimed letter is strictly larger than any green letter and any unprimed purple letter already added to
U_{x}
, and simultaneously, the primed purple letter is
strictly smaller than any primed purple letter already added to
V_{x}
.

By construction, the pair
(U_{x},V_{x})
of inner and outer standard tableaux is the same for any vertex of
\textsf{B}^{x}
. More
precisely,
U_{x}
of
shape
\mu^{x}
is the extension of
U_{0}
filled with the alphabet
\{{\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0,1,0}g_{|\mu|}}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{2}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x}\}
;
V_{x}
of skew shape
\nu/\nu^{x}
is filled with the alphabet of primed purple letters
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x}<\cdots<p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+\cdots+x_{q}}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+x_{s}}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+1}<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}^{\prime}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{1}.
Regarding
U_{x}
, extend the column
r
of
U_{0}
with the
x_{r}
unprimed purple letters
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}
, the column
s
with the
x_{s}
unprimed purple letters
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+x_{s}}
, and finally the column
t
with the
x_{t}
unprimed purple letters
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+\cdots+x_{q}+1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}+\cdots+x_{q}+x_{t}}=p_{x}
; regarding
V_{x}
of skew shape
\nu/\nu^{x}
, start with the skew shape
\nu/\mu_{0}
, and fill the
bottom

x_{r}
boxes of column
r
with the alphabet of primed purple letters
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{r}}^{\prime}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{1}
, the bottom
x_{s}
of column
s
with
the alphabet
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+x_{s}}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+1}
, and, finally, the bottom
x_{t}
boxes of column
t
with the alphabet
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p^{\prime}_{x_{r}+x_{s}+\cdots+x_{q}+1}
. See the triple
(U_{x},H^{x},V_{x})
in Figure
2
.

Figure 2.
The triple
(U_{x},H^{x},V_{x})
with
H^{x}
in gray,
V_{x}
in purple, and
U_{0}(\subseteq U_{x})
in green.

I.3
-
Rectification of the
C_{n-j+1}
crystal
B^{x}
and reduced rectification of
T_{[\pm j,n]}

Consider the triple of tableaux
(U_{x},H^{x},V_{x})
previously defined. Apply complete
SJDT_{j}
slides successively to the cells of
U_{x}
, from the largest entry to the smallest one, to rectify

H^{x}
. At the end of each complete
SJDT_{j}
slide, we get an outer cell filled with the letter where the slide started in
U_{x}
. While
H^{x}
is being rectified, the cells of
U_{x}
are slid to end up as outer corners and added to the skew standard tableau
V_{x}
.

The rectification of
H^{x}
does not depend on the choice of the inner corner made in each step during the rectification process

[
lec02
, Corollary 6.3.9]
. Applying
SJDT_{j}
to any corner of
U_{x}
in an element of
\textsf{B}^{x}
(recall that for all elements
of
\textsf{B}^{x}
,
U_{x}

is the same) gives a crystal isomorphism. This observation is equivalent to the fact that the rectification does not depend on the filling of
U_{x}
:
U_{x}
is a choice to keep track of the rectification of
H^{x}
(or of any other vertex of
\textsf{B}^{x}
). If a complete
SJDT_{j}
slide applies to
an inner corner of
H^{x}
, then a complete
SJDT_{j}
slide also applies to the same inner corner in every vertex of the crystal

\textsf{B}^{x}
and creates the same outer corner filled with the same letter.

However, if the number of boxes of
H^{x}
,
|H^{x}|
, exceeds the minimal number of boxes of its Knuth class, it will be necessary to apply

SJDT_{j}
more than
|U_{x}|=|\mu|+x
times to rectify
H^{x}
. When
H^{x}
has the minimal number of boxes of its Knuth class, only
x
unprimed purple letters and
|\mu|
green letters will slide outwards and join the outer tableau
V_{x}
.

Let
2y\geq 0
be the number of boxes of
H^{x}
that exceeds the minimal number of boxes of its Knuth class, that is,

2y=|H^{x}|-|\textsf{rectification}_{j}(H^{x})|.

When
H^{x}
has the minimal number of boxes of its Knuth class,
y=0
. Necessarily
2y
boxes of
H^{x}
will be lost in the
SJDT_{j}
rectification process. Henceforth, the
SJDT_{j}
B.2 case will be applied
y
times, each application creates a non- admissible column followed by the application of a contractor
R3
operation resulting in the loss of two boxes.

Remark 14
.

Theorem 6.1.9 in Lecouvey’s paper
[
lec02
]
says: if the B.2 case appears with the creation of a non-admissible column when applying complete SJDT to an inner corner of a KN skew tableau, it has to be at the initial column where the inner corner was originally contained.

This observation implies that each of the
y
mentioned non-admissible columns will only occur in the columns containing the inner corners where the slide started.

The complete
SJDT_{j}
slides applied successively to the entries of
U_{x}
, as mentioned, will transform the crystal
\textsf{B}^{x}

into an isomorphic crystal of KN skew tableaux, as long as the
SJDT_{j}
B.2 case does not create a non-admissible column.
Otherwise, one has an isomorphic crystal where each vertex has a non-admissible column. In this case, we apply the contractor operator
R3
to that column in each vertex, erasing a pair
(k,\bar{k})
if
k\in[\pm j,n]
is the lowest entry such that
N(k)>k
.
Then, as in
\bf I.2
above, the cells from the top and the bottom of the current column are emptied and the remaining entries
are placed in order. We get a new isomorphic crystal of KN skew tableaux where each vertex has two fewer boxes. As observed above, this may only happen in the
y
columns where
SJDT_{j}
was applied, specifically, those containing the inner corners where the slides started; no other boxes are deleted in the rectification process of
\textsf{B}^{x}
.

Eventually,
H^{x}
is rectified to
\textsf{rectification}(H^{x})
, as are all vertices of
\textsf{B}^{x}
, and we get the crystal
R
of straight KN
tableaux with highest weight element
\textsf{rectification}(H^{x})
,

\textsf{B}^{0}\simeq\textsf{B}^{x}\simeq\textsf{R}.

I.3.1
-

of

Remark 15
.

In our construction, purple letters are larger than all green ones (
46
). However, for the red ones together with the two other colors, we just write (
47
).

I.4
-
The Schützenberger-Lusztig involution on the
C_{n-j+1}
crystals
\textsf{B}^{0}
its rectification, the crystal
R
, and the reversal
.

Let
{L}^{0}
be the lowest weight element of the
C_{n-j+1}
connected normal crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
. The crystal

R
with highest weight element
\textsf{rectification}_{j}(H^{0})
is the rectification of the crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
and contains

\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
.
Let

F
be the composition of the sequence of lowering operators connecting
H^{0}
to
T_{[\pm j,n]}
in
\textsf{B}^{0}
,
F(H^{0})=T_{[\pm j,n]}
.
The Schützenberger-Lusztig involution
ξ
in
\textsf{B}^{0}
gives
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xi}{{{}}\xi}(T_{[\pm j,n]})=F^{-1}(L^{0})
, where
F^{-1}
means the sequence
obtained by replacing each lowering operator
f_{i}
in
F
with the corresponding raising operator
e_{i}
. In each crystal of the sequence (
48
), (
49
), (
50
) above, the same sequence
F
(
F^{-1}
) connects the corresponding highest (lowest) weight element to the corresponding coplactic image of
T_{[\pm j,n]}
(
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{xi}{{{}}\xi}(T_{[\pm j,n]}
)). In particular,
F
connects

\textsf{rectification}_{j}(H^{0})
to
\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{\pm j,n})
,
F(\textsf{rectification}_{j}H^{0})=\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
.
By Santos
[
sa21a
]
, the Schützenberger-Lusztig involution in
R
guarantees that

\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})=F^{-1}(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(L^{0})
is in
R
.
Thanks to the crystal isomorphisms and Lemma
5
,

\displaystyle\textsf{{reversal}}^{C_{n-j+1}}(T_{[\pm j,n]})=F^{-1}(L^{0})=\textsf{arectification}_{j}\,\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})).

(51)

To compute the reversal of
T_{[\pm j,n]}
in
\textsf{B}^{0}
without using the sequence
F
of crystal operators and the highest/lowest weight elements
H^{0}
,

L_{0}
of
\textsf{B}^{0}
, we use Santos’ evacuation on
\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
and the rectification sequence of crystals backwards in (

), (

),(

) stored in the standard skew tableau

.

Step II
.
Computation of symplectic evacuation of

\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
in the
{C_{n-j+1}}
crystal
R
.

The tableau
\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
is admissible in the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. Use Santos’ algorithm as follows: take
\pi
-rotation and change the sign of
\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
; then, apply
SJDT_{j}
to obtain
\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]}))
in
the crystal
R
.

Replace the tableau pair
(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})),V)
with

(\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})),V).

Step III
.
Symplectic reversal of
T_{[\pm j,n]}
in the
{C_{n-j+1}}
crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
.

Consider the pair of tableaux
(\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rectification}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})),V)
, where
V
is the standard tableau consisting of all the slid letters in the rectification sequence (
48
), (
49
),(
50
) on the alphabet of green, purple and red letters.

Apply the
reverse

SJDT_{j}
,
RSJDT_{j}
, to the entries of
V
from the smallest to the largest to send
\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]}))
to

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{rev}{{{}}\textsf{reversal}}(T_{[\pm j,n]})=F^{-1}(L_{0})
in the
{C_{n-j+1}}
crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
.

When the
SJDT_{j}
applies to an unprimed red letter
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r_{i}
,
i\in\{1,\dots,y\}
, in
V
, the letter
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r_{i}
slides to the top of a column with the cell
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r^{\prime}_{i}
on the bottom. At this point, we have reached the crystal
\textsf{B}^{x,i}
. Then we apply the dilation operator
R3
to the column containing the pair
(\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r_{i},\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r^{\prime}_{i})
by erasing those entries and adding a pair of symmetric entries
(k,\bar{k})
so that we get a non-admissible column on the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
. The
SJDT_{j}
applies now to the next letter bigger than
\color[rgb]{1,0,0}r^{\prime}_{i}
. In this complete reverse slide, the
SJDT_{j}
B.2 case occurs.

When the reverse
SJDT_{j}
slides have been applied to all non-primed purple letters, we have reached the crystal
\textsf{B}^{x}
, where the columns

r,s,\dots,t
have
x_{i}
non-primed purple letters
\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{1}+\cdots+x_{i-1}+1}<\cdots<\color[rgb]{0.75,0,0.25}p_{x_{1}+\cdots+x_{i}}
on the top and the
corresponding primed letters on the bottom for
i=r,s,\dots,t
. Then, for
i=t,\dots,s,r
, we apply the dilation operator
R3
to each such column
i

x_{i}
times, and we reach the crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
, where each vertex has non-admissible columns
r,s,\dots,t
. In
particular, we obtain
\textsf{reversal}^{C_{n-j+1}}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
.

Step IV
.
Partial symplectic reversal of
T
computes
\xi^{C_{n}}_{[j,n]}(T)
.

\xi^{C_{n}}_{[j,n]}(T)
is the Schützenberger–Lusztig involution of
T=(T^{+}_{[j-1]},T_{[\pm j,n]},T^{-}_{[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]})

in the crystal connected component
\textsf{B}\approx\textsf{B}^{0}
of
\textsf{KN}_{[j,n]}(\lambda,n)
.
Replace
T_{[\pm j,n]}
with
\textsf{reversal}^{C_{n-j+1}}(T_{[\pm j,n]})
, (
51
), in
T
,

which gives

\displaystyle\xi_{[j,n]}^{C_{n}}(T)=\textsf{reversal}^{C_{n}}_{[j,n]}(T)=(T^{+}_{[j-1]},\textsf{arect}_{j}\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[\pm j,n]})),T^{-}_{[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]}).

(52)

Remark 16
.

•

If we put
j=1
in the colorful algorithm, we reduce to Step II of
C_{n}
evacuation.

•

If
T\in\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{ssyt}{{{}}\textsf{SSYT}(\lambda,n)}\subseteq\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)}
, our colorful tableau switching algorithm reduces to just the green color, that is, to
the
ordinary tableau switching
of the tableau-pair
(U_{0},T_{[j,n]})
, with
U_{0}
a standard tableau of shape
\mu
and

T_{[j,n]}
a semi-standard skew tableau of shape
\lambda/\mu
on the alphabet
[j,n]
. The semi-standard skew-tableau
T_{[j,n]}
on
the alphabet
[j,n]
is also a
C_{n-j+1}
admissible tableau.
R3
does not apply, and
SJDT_{j}
reduces to the ordinary JDT.
Therefore, purple and red colors do not pop up in Step I. This means Step I returns the pair
(\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[j,n]},V)
, with

\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[j,n]})
a semi-standard tableau in the alphabet
[j,n]
with
|\lambda|-|\mu|
boxes and
V
completely green.
Step II computes the symplectic
C_{n-j+1}
evacuation of
\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[j,n]})
; this step produces a semi-standard tableau of
the same shape on the alphabet
[\bar{n},\bar{j}]
. Step III applies the ordinary reverse JDT to

\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[j,n]})
governed by the green
V
and returns a semi-standard skew tableau of shape

\lambda/\mu
on the alphabet
[\bar{n},\bar{j}]
. Step IV computes the partial reversal
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{jsymprev}{{{}}\textsf{reversal}^{C_{n}}_{J}}(T)
,
J=[j,n]
, with

T^{-}_{[\overline{j-1},\bar{1}]}
empty,

\displaystyle\xi_{[j,n]}^{C_{n}}(T)=\textsf{reversal}^{C_{n}}_{[j,n]}(T)=(T^{+}_{[j-1]},\textsf{arect}_{j}\textsf{evac}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\textsf{rect}_{j}(T_{[j,n]}))).

(53)

•

The algorithm for the full
C_{m}
reversal of a KN skew tableau
T\in\textsf{KN}(\lambda/\mu,m)
results from our colorful tableau
switching algorithm by considering the image of
T
,
(T_{\mu},\hat{T})
, in the full sub-crystal

\textsf{B}(\mu,\lambda)\subseteq\textsf{KN}_{[j,n]}(\lambda,n)
, where
n=m+j-1
(Subsection
7.4.2
). Let
B
be the crystal connected
component of
\textsf{B}(\mu,\lambda)

containing
(T_{\mu},\hat{T})
, where
T_{\mu}
is the Yamanouchi tableau of shape
\mu
and
\hat{T}
is obtained by increasing
each of the entries
of
T
by
j-1
. Then, restricting
(T_{\mu},\hat{T})
to the alphabet
[\pm j,n]
,
\hat{T}
is an admissible
C_{n-j+1}
skew
tableau in the

C_{n-j+1}
crystal
\textsf{B}^{0}
. Our algorithm reduces to Step I with just green and red, Step II and Step III. Finally, we subtract

j-1
from the entries of
\textsf{reversal}^{C_{n-j+1}}(\hat{T})
to get
\textsf{reversal}^{C_{m}}(T)
. However, subtraction by
j-1

cancels the last step in the reduced
SJDT_{j}
(Definition
4
), and therefore it is enough to apply
SJDT.

This means that the algorithm for the full
C_{m}
reversal of the KN skew tableau
T
results from our algorithm with

\textsf{B}^{0}=\textsf{B}(T)
a type
C_{m}
crystal,
x=0
and applying SJDT to
U_{0}
to get
(rect(T),V)
, where
V
is a skew
standard
tableau without purple letters. Then RSJDT applied to
V
gives

\textsf{reversal}^{C_{m}}(T)=\textsf{arectification}\,\textsf{evac}^{C_{m}}(\textsf{rectification}(T)).

. We have
\textsf{wt}(P)=(-1,1,-2,,1)
. To compute
\xi_{[2,4]}^{C_{4}}(P)=\textsf{reversal}_{[2,4]}^{C_{4}}(P)
, we freeze the letters
1,\bar{1}
in
P
and consider
P_{[\pm 2,4]}
.
P_{[\pm 2,4]}
is not an admissible
C_{3}
tableau in the alphabet
[\pm 2,4]
: the second column
24\bar{2}
is not an admissible
C_{3}
column;
24\bar{2}\overset{SJDT_{2}}{\rightarrow}4
. The column reading of
P_{[\pm 2,4]}
is
2\bar{3}24\bar{2}4\bar{4}\bar{3}\overset{R_{3}}{\equiv}2\bar{3}44\bar{4}\bar{3}
. We include this non-admissible second column in the
SJDT_{2}
sequence to rectify
P_{[\pm 2,4]}
.

9.4.
General Dynkin sub-diagram and virtualization

Let
\xi^{C_{n}}_{[1,j]}
,
1\leq j\leq n-1
, be the Schützenberger–Lusztig involution on
\textsf{KN}_{[1,j]}(\lambda,n)
.

\textsf{KN}_{[1,j]}(\lambda,n)
is a type
A_{j}
crystal of Kashiwara–Nakashima tableaux of shape
\lambda
on the alphabet
[\pm n]
with
lowering and raising operators
f_{i}
and
e_{i}
, respectively, given by the type
C_{n}
signature rule with
i\in[1,j]
. Notice that the unique
crystal operators which change the signs of the entries are
f_{n}
and
e_{n}
, which are forgotten. Next we give a computation of
\xi^{C_{n}}_{[p,q]}
, for any
1\leq p\leq q\leq n
, via virtualization
E
and bring it back to
\textsf{KN}_{[p,q]}(\lambda,n)
by applying
E^{-1}
, see Theorem
5
and Theorem
6
below.

In the next section, we give a computation of
\xi^{C_{n}}_{[1,j]}
,
1\leq j\leq n-1
, via virtualization
E
and bring it back to
\textsf{KN}_{[1,j]}(\lambda,n)
by applying
E^{-1}
. See Theorem
5
below.

9.4.1.
Embedding of a partial symplectic Schützenberger–Lusztig involution and back.

Let
J\subseteq[n]
be a
sub-Dynkin diagram of the type
C_{n}
Dynkin diagram
I=[n]
. Let
U
be a connected component of the Levi branched crystal
KN

J

(λ,n)
with
J\subseteq[n]
and with highest and lowest weight elements
u^{\textsf{high}}
and
u^{\textsf{low}}
, respectively. Recall from Subsection
5.2
, Proposition
2
, that each connected component
U
of the Levi branched crystal
\textsf{KN}_{J}(\lambda,n)
is embedded via
E
into a connected component of the Levi branched crystal
\textsf{SSYT}_{J\cup\bar{J}}(\lambda,n)
with highest and lowest weight elements
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{high}})
and
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{low}})
, respectively.

Let
P=(P^{+},P^{-})\in\textsf{SSYT}(\lambda^{A},n,\bar{n})
. The crystals

\textsf{SSYT}_{[p,q]}(\lambda^{A},n,\bar{n})
and
\textsf{SSYT}_{[\overline{q+1},\overline{p+1}]}(\lambda^{A},n,\bar{n})
are isomorphic to
\textsf{SSYT}_{[p,q]}(\lambda^{A}_{+},n)
and
\textsf{SSYT}_{[\overline{q+1},\overline{p+1}]}(\lambda^{A}/\lambda^{A}_{+},\bar{n})
, respectively (recall Remark
5
). The corresponding pair of isomorphic crystals has the same multiset of highest
weight vectors in
\mathbb{Z}^{q-p+1}
respectively, regarding the sub-Dynkin diagram
[p,q]
. We may then write

\displaystyle\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[\overline{q+1},\overline{p+1}]}(P)=(\xi^{A_{n-1}}_{[p,q]}(P^{+}),\xi^{A_{n-1}}_{[\overline{q+1},\overline{p+1}]}(P^{-})).

(54)

Theorem 5
.

Let
T\in\textsf{KN}_{[p,q]}(\lambda,n)

.

Moreover,

\xi^{C_{n}}_{[p,q]}(T)={E^{-1}\textsf{reversal}^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]}\textsf{reversal}^{A_{2n-1}}_{[\overline{q+1},\overline{p+1}]}E}(T).

and (

). Then it follows from Theorem

.

Theorem 6
.

Let

of type

. Then

(λ

)
,

, and

\displaystyle\xi^{C_{n}}_{[k,n]}=E^{-1}\textsf{reversal}^{A_{2n-1}}_{[k,2n-k]}E.

(56)

Proof.

Recall Corollary
2
and that, in the case of the branched crystal
\textsf{SSYT}_{[k,2n-k]}(\lambda,n,\bar{n})
,

\theta(i)=2n-i
, for

. Let

, and
let the highest and lowest weight

be

and

, respectively.

T=f_{i_{r}}\dots f_{i_{1}}(u^{\textsf{high}}),i_{1},\dots,i_{r}\in[k,n],

\displaystyle\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T)=f^{A}_{i_{r}}f^{A}_{2n-i_{r}}\dots f^{A}_{i_{1}}f^{A}_{2n-i_{1}}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{high}})),i_{1},\dots,i_{r}\in[k,n],

(57)

and

Then, from Subsection

, (

),

\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\xi^{C_{n}}_{[k,n]}(T))=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(e_{i_{r}}\dots e_{i_{1}}(u^{\textsf{low}}))=e^{A}_{i_{r}}e^{A}_{2n-i_{r}}\dots e^{A}_{i_{1}}e^{A}_{2n-i_{1}}\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{low}})

and, from (

),

\displaystyle\xi^{A}_{[k,2n-k]}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T))

\displaystyle=e^{A}_{\theta(i_{r})}e^{A}_{\theta(2n-i_{r})}\dots e^{A}_{\theta(i_{1})}e^{A}_{\theta(2n-i_{1})}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{low}}))

\displaystyle=e^{A}_{2n-i_{r}}e^{A}_{i_{r}}\dots e^{A}_{2n-i_{1}}e^{A}_{i_{1}}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{low}}))

\displaystyle=e^{A}_{i_{r}}e^{A}_{2n-i_{r}}\dots e^{A}_{i_{1}}e^{A}_{2n-i_{1}}\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(u^{\textsf{low}})=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\xi^{C}_{[k,n]}(T)).

Finally, (

Using a generalized form of Lemma
6
, the following corollary is a generalization of the two theorems above.

Corollary 4
.

Let
T\in\textsf{KN}_{[p,q]\cup[k,n]}(\lambda,n)
of subtype
A_{p-q+1}\times C_{n-k+1}
for some

1\leq p\leq q<k-1<n
. Then

\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]\cup[2n-q,2n-p]\cup[k,2n-k]}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T))=\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[2n-q,2n-p]}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[k,2n-k]}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T))=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\xi^{C_{n}}_{[p,q]\cup[k,n]}(T)).

Moreover, on
SSYT
(λ

A

,n,
¯

n

)
,
\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]}=\textsf{reversal}^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]}
,

\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[k,2n-k]}=\textsf{reversal}^{A_{2n-1}}_{[k,2n-k]}
and

\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[2n-q,2n-p]}=\textsf{reversal}^{A_{2n-1}}_{[2n-q,2n-p]}.

Remark 17
.

Both
\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[p,q]\cup[2n-q,2n-p]}
and
\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{[k,2n-k]}
act on the set
SSYT
(λ

A

,n,
¯

n

)
to define a permutation such that the subset
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)})
is preserved. In other words, each of these involutions defines a permutation on
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)})
when their action is restricted to this subset.

Corollary 5
.

Let
\textsf{SSYT}(\mu,2n)
with
\mu
a partition with at most
2n
parts, and let
B_{n}
be the Weyl group realized as
<r_{i}=(i\;i+1)(2n-i\;2n-i+1),\,r_{n}=(n\;n+1),\;1\leq i\leq n-1>
. Then
\{\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i},\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{n},1\leq i\leq n-1\}
define an action of
B_{n}
on
\textsf{SSYT}(\lambda,2n)
by
r_{i}.b=\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i}b
,
1\leq i\leq n-1
, and
r_{n}.b=\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}.b
, for
b\in\textsf{SSYT}(\lambda,2n)
such that

(1)

e^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}e^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i}=\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i}f^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}f^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i}
,
1\leq i<n
,

(2)

e^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}e^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}=\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}f^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}f^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}
,

(3)

\textsf{wt}(r_{i}.b)=r_{\textsf{iwt}}(b)
,
1\leq i\leq n
,

(4)

w_{0}.T_{\mu}=T^{\textsf{low}}_{\mu}
,
w_{0}=(r_{n}\cdots r_{1})^{n}
the long element of
B_{n}
.

(5)

if
\mu=\lambda^{A}
is the virtual partition for some
\lambda
, it preserves the action of
B_{n}
on the underlying set of the crystal
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)})
embedded in the crystal
\textsf{SSYT}(\lambda^{A},2n)
.

Proof.

(5)

5
and Theorem
6
with

and respectively

that
\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{p}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{\overline{p+1}}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T))=\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{p}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-p}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T))=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\xi^{C_{n}}_{p}(T))
and
\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{n}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(T))=\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\xi^{C_{n}}_{n}(T))
.
From Proposition
6
,
3
,
\{\xi^{C_{n}}_{n}:1\leq i\leq n\}
define an action of

on
KN
(λ,n)
. Therefore
\{\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{i}\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{2n-i},\xi^{A_{2n-1}}_{n},1\leq i\leq n-1\}
is the translation of this action to the embedded crystal
\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{E}{{{}}E}(\lx@glossaries@gls@link{main}{symptab}{{{}}\textsf{KN}(\lambda,n)})
in

.
∎
